## Review

**7 out of 10, Effective.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck has a consistent black and NVIDIA green identity, action-style titles, and charts that carry real arguments, notably the paired curves on slide 10, the per-GW bars on slide 11 and the indexed networking chart on slide 17. The route from platform to economics to products to residual value to a closing summary is mostly coherent. Weaknesses are consequential: many slides depend on small gray body text and tiny labels (slides 4, 8, 13, 18, 21, 22), the financial tables on slide 9 are unreadable at presentation size, and slide 2 is a dense disclaimer wall. The deck reads better as a pre-read than as a projected presentation, and the closing slide 28 is a plain text block that ends weakly.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the paired comparison charts on slide 10, the green-against-gray emphasis with annotated multiples, and the single-chart-with-numbered-list layout on slide 17.

**Most useful repair:** Enlarge or simplify the small body text and diagram labels on slides 4, 8, 13, 18 and 21, and replace the screenshot-style tables on slide 9 with a few legible headline figures.

**What would change the score:** I would rebuild slide 28 as a visual summary tied to the three growth pillars, and cut the intro paragraphs above the charts on slides 11 and 15 to one line each.

_Confidence medium. 29 of 29 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck is NVIDIA's September 2026 non-deal roadshow presentation for investors. It opens with a title slide and a long safe-harbor disclaimer covering forward-looking statements and non-GAAP measures, then moves into the company overview, describing NVIDIA as a full-stack AI factory platform and setting out its unique position across workload breadth, co-design, the CUDA ecosystem, deployment and global reach. A recent-events slide lists announcements since the last earnings report, followed by sections on capital returns and strategic investments, the full-stack platform, ecosystem customers, financial capacity and commitments, token economics, and product segments including Vera CPU, Groq 3 LPX, networking, NVLink Fusion and CUDA. Later sections cover residual value of GPUs, open models, Nemotron, physical AI and a closing summary of compounding growth. Slides worth studying include the per-gigawatt revenue chart on slide 11, the token economics comparison on slide 10, the networking growth chart on slide 17 and the GPU residual value curve on slide 22, all of which use charts to carry the argument.

## Questions about this deck

**What does the NVIDIA deck say about its per-gigawatt revenue opportunity?**

Slide 11 says NVIDIA's per gigawatt revenue opportunity, based on its reference design, has expanded from $18 billion during the Hopper generation to $25 billion with Blackwell and now $40 billion with Rubin. The slide ties this growth to advances in the GPU and a broader portfolio that includes stand-alone CPU racks, LPU racks and scale-in networking.

**How many slides does the NVIDIA non-deal roadshow presentation have?**

The NVIDIA deck has 29 pages. It opens with a title slide on slide 1 and a safe-harbor disclaimer on slide 2, then moves into the company overview.

**What does the NVIDIA deck say about its share repurchase authorization?**

Slide 6 says NVIDIA announced on September 28 a $150 billion increase to its share repurchase authorization, bringing the total authorization to $235 billion. The company expects to utilize this authorization through FY28.

**What does the NVIDIA deck say about its networking revenue growth?**

Slide 17 says that since the acquisition of Mellanox in 2020, NVIDIA's Networking revenue has grown at a CAGR of 70%, materially outperforming industry peers. The slide's chart shows the total market CAGR for CY2020-2025 at 28%.

### Slide 3

**Text**

NVIDIA
NVIDIA’s invention of the GPU in 1999 sparked the growth of the PC gaming market, redefined computer graphics, revolutionized accelerated computing, and ignited the era of modern AI.
NVIDIA has evolved from a chip provider to today, a full-stack AI factory platform – spanning GPUs, CPUs, networking, systems, software, developer tools, and cloud-scale deployment.
Today, NVIDIA is driving one of the most important computing platform shifts in decades as it builds the intelligence infrastructure layer for the global AI economy.
Every major technology era creates a new foundational layer. Electricity required power grids. The internet required networks, data centers, and cloud platforms. AI requires a new kind of infrastructure: AI factories that convert data and energy into intelligence – monetizable tokens.
As software, systems, robots, factories, vehicles, and enterprises become AI-powered, they will need infrastructure that is fast, trusted, scalable, widely adopted, fungible, and durable. NVIDIA is the full-stack infrastructure platform for the age of AI: the company that turns chips, systems, software, developers, and data centers into AI factories.
Headquarters: Santa Clara, CA | Employees: 42,000+

**Layout**

Full-bleed photograph covers the whole slide. A single text panel occupies the right 58% of the slide, starting at 20.2% from the top and running to 88.4%, with its left edge at 42% of the width. All text is left-aligned inside the panel at a left inset of about 3.2% of the width, with the title at the top of the panel and the headquarters line as the last line. The text is set in one column with consistent paragraph gaps. There is no visible footer, page number, or logo.

**Visuals**

One photograph filling the full slide as background. Subject: a modern campus outdoor space with a large open steel space-frame canopy of crossing white-grey beams and diagonal struts, a round tiered structure with warm terracotta-coloured walls beneath it, a glass building wing on the left, and landscaped lawns, pathways, stone benches and trees in the foreground. The photo is heavily blurred (depth-of-field and motion softness) and viewed from an elevated angle. Lighting is soft natural daylight with warm late-sun tones on the trees and pale blue sky at the top left. Dominant colours: sky blue, pale grey-white steel, green foliage, warm tan and terracotta. The image is not cropped in a way that matters, is full-bleed, and has a dark overlay panel on its right side. No logos or icons.

**Design**

Background is a blurred outdoor photograph with a solid black base layer. A semi-transparent black panel (#000000 at 75% opacity) sits over the right part of the photo and carries all text. Title text is white (#FFFFFF) in NVIDIASans Medium, a geometric humanist sans-serif, regular case, at about 5.4% of slide height. ### Slide 4

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: many slides depend on small gray body text and tiny labels (slides 4, 8, 13, 18, 21, 22), the financial tables on slide 9 are unreadable at presentation size, and slide 2 is a dense disclaimer wall.
- Suggested repair: Enlarge or simplify the small body text and diagram labels on slides 4, 8, 13, 18 and 21, and replace the screenshot-style tables on slide 9 with a few legible headline figures.

**Layout**

Five equal-width columns of cards sit in a single row beneath a centred title and a centred two-line subtitle, all on a black field. The title is centred near the top at about 10.5% of slide height, with the subtitle beneath it. Each card has an icon at its top, a green heading, a white centred body paragraph, a green chevron, and a second centred white paragraph at the bottom. Outer margins are about 5% left and right. The NVIDIA logo sits at the bottom right. There is no footer text and no slide number.

**Visuals**

Five line icons in lime green #BFEB2E with 2px-equivalent strokes, one per card. Card 1: a bar chart of four vertical outlined bars of varying heights with a rising zigzag line above them and a circled dollar sign at the top right of the line. Card 2: a circled dollar sign with an arrow curving clockwise from its upper right edge. Card 3: a browser-style window outline with a top bar and three small dashes at the right, containing a centred code symbol with angle brackets and a slash. Card 4: a factory silhouette with a sawtooth roof, a small square window block at lower left, three vertical tick marks, and a tall slanted chimney with a circle near its top. Card 5: a globe drawn as an outlined circle with a vertical ellipse and horizontal latitude lines. Three of the five icons are not cropped and sit fully inside their cards. Below each icon is a chevron, a downward-pointing V in green #76B900. No photographs, charts or tables.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 covering the full slide.

### Slide 6

**Text**

NVIDIA Announces a $150B Share Repurchase Authorization Increase / NVIDIA makes strategic investments across the ecosystem to develop and accelerate growth of the market and CUDA ecosystem. Today, NVIDIA's portfolio consists of / 13 public companies and 229 private companies. Realized ROI on exits-to-date exceeds 3X. We are committed to sharing the company's success with shareholders and / will return excess free cash flow net of strategic uses in the form of share repurchases and a gradually growing dividend. On September 28, we announced a $150 / billion increase to our share repurchase authorization, bringing our total authorization to $235 billion, which we expect to utilize through FY28. / Strategic Investments Across All Layers of the AI Cake / Applications / ABRIDGE / Agility Robotics / NOKIA / Orbital Industries / Revolut / SYNOPSYS / WAYVE / Models / ANTHROPIC / Mistral AI / OpenAI / SPACEX / ssi / THINKING MACHINES / Infrastructure / CoreWeave / Crusoe / firmus / Lambda / NEBIUS / NSCALE / VOLTA / Chips / COHERENT / CORNING / intel. / LUMENTUM / MARVELL / MEDIATEK / SiFive / Energy / LPS / ATOMIC CANYON / LANCIUM / REDWOOD MATERIALS / SB Energy / TerraPower / Note: Strategic investments are not exclusive to those included in the table. / Increasing Stock Repurchases ($ Billions) / $250 / $235 / $200 / $150 / FY27: $53B YTD / Already / $13B above / full-year FY26 / New Authorization, / $150 / $100 / $50 / $40 / $34 / $10 / $0 / FY24 / FY25 / FY26 / FY27 YTD / Total / Remaining Prior / Authorization, / $85 / Authorization

**Layout**

Black full-bleed slide with a centred single-line title at the top (about 10.5% from the top) spanning nearly the full width, followed by a centred four-line grey intro paragraph. Below, two equal rounded-outline panels sit side by side on a two-column grid: left panel (about x 5 to 49, y 31 to 86) holds the logo grid, right panel (about x 51 to 95, y 31 to 86) holds a bar chart. A small NVIDIA logo sits bottom right at about x 93 to 98, y 94 to 96; no slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Left panel: a logo matrix in five groups, each with a green category label centred above a row of white logos separated by thin grey horizontal rules. Applications (7 logos): Abridge, Agility Robotics, Nokia, Orbital Industries, Revolut, Synopsys, Wayve. Models (6 logos): Anthropic, Mistral AI, OpenAI, SpaceX, SSI, Thinking Machines. Infrastructure (7 logos): CoreWeave, Crusoe, firmus, Lambda, Nebius, Nscale, Volta. Chips (7 logos): Coherent, Corning, Intel, Lumentum, Marvell, MediaTek, SiFive. Energy / LPS (5 logos): Atomic Canyon, Lancium, Redwood Materials, SB Energy, TerraPower. All logos are white or light grey on black. Right panel: a vertical bar chart titled 'Increasing Stock Repurchases ($ Billions)', y-axis $0 to $250 in steps of $50 with labels, x-axis categories FY24, FY25, FY26, FY27 YTD, Total Authorization. Series values as printed: FY24 $10, FY25 $34, FY26 $40, FY27 YTD $53 (highlighted in bright lime #B8FF3C with a callout box), Total Authorization $235 (stacked: New Authorization $150 in deep purple #351147 on top, Remaining Prior Authorization $85 in darker purple at the base). Bars for FY24 to FY26 are dark olive green #3B5D00; the FY27 YTD bar is lime green. A rounded callout box with lime outline (about x 75 to 90, y 51 to 64) reads 'FY27: $53B YTD / Already $13B above full-year FY26', connected by a thin lime leader line from the box to the top of the FY27 YTD bar. No legend; the note about the stacked total is in-chart text labels.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 covering the entire slide. Pure black background (#000000). Titles and primary text white (#FFFFFF); intro paragraph and footnote grey (#A1A1A1 and #757575); accent category labels and highlights NVIDIA green (#76B900) and lime (#B8FF3C). Typeface NVIDIASans, a geometric grotesque sans-serif: Medium for titles and category labels, Regular for body, Bold for highlighted figures. Title is about 4.9% of slide height, intro and chart labels about 1.7 to 2.0%. Panels have thin grey (#5A5A5A approx.) rounded outlines with small corner radius and no fill or shadow. Bars are flat with no gradient.

### Slide 7

**Text**

NVIDIA Full-Stack AI Factory Platform,
Not the Chip, Determines Economic Output
What NVIDIA builds is fundamentally different from
what other chip companies build.
The Vera Rubin platform integrates seven purpose-
built chips across five rack-scale systems. And,
through the DSX AI factory platform, NVIDIA helps
design, build and operate efficient, resilient and
grid-ready AI factories.
The ability to extreme co-design at AI factory scale
is unique to NVIDIA, and the result is integrated yet
customizable infrastructure that can be deployed
across every major cloud, inside enterprise data
centers, at the edge, and into robotics and physical
AI systems.
NVIDIA delivers x-factor performance gains every year
and continuously improves the performance of its
installed base through software enhancements.

**Layout**

Single-slide black layout with a centered two-line title across the top, a left text column of body copy starting at about 31% of slide height, and a bordered framed graphic panel occupying the right half from about 38% to 95% horizontally and 28.5% to 85.6% vertically. The left column and panel are roughly top-aligned at the content area, with the left text column left-aligned at a margin of about 5.2% of slide width. The NVIDIA logo sits bottom right at about 93% x and 94.5% y. No footer text and no slide number are visible.

**Visuals**

A single framed panel image (about 57.3% by 57.3% of slide, 16:9 image of 3840 by 2160 px) with a near-black background and a thin light grey rounded border. Its title reads The NVIDIA Full-Stack AI Factory in white sans-serif, centered near the top. The upper part has three groups of icons with captions. Left group, captioned CUDA-X Libraries in gold, shows a row of six overlapping rectangular tiles with green, teal and blue computer-graphics renderings, the central tile brightest with a glowing blue and green circuit image. Centre group, captioned Platform Libraries in gold, is a grid of labelled grey rounded rectangles: top row cuDNN; second row NIXL, CCCL, DOCA; third row cuSPARSE, NVSHMEM, CUDA Python, NCCL, NVTX; fourth row cuBLAS, TensorRT-LLM, cuTILE Python, Megatron Core, NVRTC; fifth row Transformer Engine, CUTLASS, Dynamo; sixth row CuTe DSL. Right group, captioned Open Models in gold, shows six overlapping tiles with figurative renderings: a globe, a DNA-like helix, a central humanoid figure, a spiral and a human body figure. Below is a row of seven line-drawn server rack illustrations in glowing gold and white outline, each with a small two-line caption above: Vera Rubin NVL72 Compute Tray (with labels Rubin GPU, Vera, Grace BlueField, ConnectX-9 pointing to the rack), Vera Rubin NVL72 NVLink Switch Tray (label NVLink 6 Switch), Vera CPU Tray (label Vera), Spectrum-6 SPX Switch Tray (label Spectrum-6 Switch), Groq 3 LPX Tray (label Groq 3), Vera BlueField-4 STX Storage Tray (labels Vera BlueField-4 STX), and Vera CPU Tray (label Vera BlueField-4). The lower band of the panel, on a dark purple-to-blue gradient, shows a large isometric rendering of a flat AI data-centre building with glowing warm white lights along its edges and a blue-lit central hall, captioned NVIDIA DSX in gold at the bottom centre. The panel has no overlay beyond the dark tint and is boxed, not cropped or full-bleed.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 with no gradient, photograph or pattern. Solid black background. Titles are white #FFFFFF in NVIDIASans Medium (a geometric humanist sans-serif), regular case, about 4.9% of slide height per line, with the two lines of the title centred. Body text is light grey #A1A1A1 in NVIDIASans Regular, sans-serif, about 2.0% of slide height, with line spacing of about 3% of slide height and paragraph gaps of about 4%. Accent colour is NVIDIA green #76B900 for the logo mark; gold labels inside the panel are about #D4B25A. The image panel has a thin grey #3A3A3A rounded border with a corner radius of about 1% of slide width. No shadows or gradients outside the panel image.

### Slide 8

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: many slides depend on small gray body text and tiny labels (slides 4, 8, 13, 18, 21, 22), the financial tables on slide 9 are unreadable at presentation size, and slide 2 is a dense disclaimer wall.
- Suggested repair: Enlarge or simplify the small body text and diagram labels on slides 4, 8, 13, 18 and 21, and replace the screenshot-style tables on slide 9 with a few legible headline figures.

### Slide 9

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: many slides depend on small gray body text and tiny labels (slides 4, 8, 13, 18, 21, 22), the financial tables on slide 9 are unreadable at presentation size, and slide 2 is a dense disclaimer wall.
- Suggested repair: Enlarge or simplify the small body text and diagram labels on slides 4, 8, 13, 18 and 21, and replace the screenshot-style tables on slide 9 with a few legible headline figures.

**Text**

NVIDIA Will Leverage Its Financial Capacity to Support the Ecosystem
Future commitments by fiscal year as of July 26, 2026, were as follows:
Remainder of 2027 / 2028 / 2029 / 2030 / 2031 / 2032 and thereafter / Total
(In billions)
Supply and capacity $ 92 $ 87 $ 88 $ 6 $ 5 $ 1 $ 279
Cloud service agreements 3 8 7 6 4 1 29
Data center leases not commenced — 1 1 2 1 20 25
Equity investments 18 3 2 2 — — 25
Capital expenditures 7 1 — — — — 8
Total $ 120 $ 100 $ 98 $ 16 $ 10 $ 22 $ 366
Future commitments by fiscal year as of July 26, 2026, were as follows:
Remainder of 2027 / 2028 / 2029 / 2030 / 2031 / 2032 and thereafter / Total
(In billions)
AI cloud agreements $ — $ 6 $ 8 $ 7 $ 6 $ 9 $ 36
Data center leases not commenced for third party — — 1 1 1 17 20
Total $ — $ 6 $ 9 $ 8 $ 7 $ 26 $ 56
The following table summarizes the maximum gross exposure related to our guarantees, including the SB Energy Corp. guarantees signed in August 2026 (in billions):
Land, power, and shell guarantees for AI clouds $ 3.5
SB Energy Corp. guarantees 105.0
Total $ 108.5
We’ve partnered with our extensive network of suppliers to secure the critical components needed to meet demand for the next several years. Our commitments increased from $119 billion last quarter to $279 billion, primarily related to the procurement of memory.
We’ve partnered with leading AI clouds to enable broader access to our AI infrastructure to serve AI startups, model builders, enterprises, research organizations and sovereign customers. Under these agreements, we will earn revenue on the upfront sale of our infrastructure and, if certain criteria are met, we will participate in revenue share generated by the AI clouds from their third-party customers.
We signed data center lease agreements with terms of ~15 years that are expected to commence between FY2028 and FY2029. We expect to reassign these data center leases to third parties.
We entered into guarantees to provide credit support on the land, power, and shell buildout to secure approximately 4.25 GW at SB Energy's PORTS-Pike Technology Campus in Ohio, which will exclusively host several generations of NVIDIA infrastructure under 20-year leases to OpenAI, subject to limited exceptions.

**Layout**

Single full-bleed black slide with the title centred in a band across the top (about 10 to 15 percent of the height) spanning nearly the full width. Three white-boxed financial tables are stacked in a left column (x about 4.8 to 47.3 percent) from top to bottom, and four green arrows lead from each table to a right-hand column of grey body paragraphs starting at x 56.4 percent. The NVIDIA logo sits at bottom right, and there is no visible slide number or footer.

**Visuals**

Four green line arrows, each with a filled triangular head pointing right toward the text. Arrow 1 is horizontal, pointing from the first table to paragraph 1. Arrow 2 is diagonal, rising from the bottom of the second table to paragraph 2. Arrow 3 is a shallow diagonal descending from the second table to paragraph 3. Arrow 4 is a shallow diagonal descending from the third table to paragraph 4. Table 1 (top, white box): caption 'Future commitments by fiscal year as of July 26, 2026, were as follows:'; columns Remainder of 2027, 2028, 2029, 2030, 2031, 2032 and thereafter, Total, with the header line '(In billions)'. Rows: Supply and capacity $92, $87, $88, $6, $5, $1, $279; Cloud service agreements 3, 8, 7, 6, 4, 1, 29; Data center leases not commenced —, 1, 1, 2, 1, 20, 25; Equity investments 18, 3, 2, 2, —, —, 25; Capital expenditures 7, 1, —, —, —, —, 8; Total $120, $100, $98, $16, $10, $22, $366. Table 2 (middle, white box): same caption and columns. Rows: AI cloud agreements $—, $6, $8, $7, $6, $9, $36; Data center leases not commenced for third party —, —, 1, 1, 1, 17, 20; Total $—, $6, $9, $8, $7, $26, $56. Table 3 (bottom, white box): caption 'The following table summarizes the maximum gross exposure related to our guarantees, including the SB Energy Corp. guarantees signed in August 2026 (in billions):'; rows Land, power, and shell guarantees for AI clouds $3.5; SB Energy Corp. guarantees 105.0; Total $108.5 (total ruled above). Table cell text is black on white with thin black rules under column headers and above totals; light grey row banding alternates on rows.

### Slide 10

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Paired side-by-side curves of tokens per megawatt and cost per million tokens make the generational comparison readable at a glance.
- Review: The deck has a consistent black and NVIDIA green identity, action-style titles, and charts that carry real arguments, notably the paired curves on slide 10, the per-GW bars on slide 11 and the indexed networking chart on slide 17.
- Worth keeping: Keep the paired comparison charts on slide 10, the green-against-gray emphasis with annotated multiples, and the single-chart-with-numbered-list layout on slide 17.

**Layout**

Two equal side-by-side chart panels sit in a two-column grid below a centred three-line header (title, subtitle, source line), with the NVIDIA logo bottom-right. The title is centred at the top of the slide, the left panel spans roughly x 5% to 49% and the right panel x 51% to 95%, both at y 31% to 86%, leaving equal side margins. No slide number or footer text is shown.

**Visuals**

Two line charts in outlined rounded-corner boxes. Left chart: title 'Tokens Per MW'; y-axis 0M to 80M in 20M steps, unlabelled units beyond the 'M' suffix; x-axis 'Interactivity (Tokens/Sec/User)' ticks 50, 100, 150, 200, 250, 300. Series 1 'Vera Rubin NVL72' (green #76B900) starts about x=84 at about 71M and falls smoothly to about 0M near x=272. Series 2 'GB300 NVL72' (grey #7F7F7F) starts about x=50 at about 58M, falls steeply to about 0M by x=170. A green dashed vertical double-headed arrow at x=150 spans from the GB300 curve to the Vera Rubin curve, labelled '30X' in green. Right chart: title 'Dollars Per Million Tokens'; y-axis $0.00 to $0.40 in $0.10 steps; x-axis 'Interactivity (Tokens/Sec/User)' ticks 50 to 300. Series 'GB300 NVL72' (grey) stays near $0.00 to about x=100, then rises steeply, exiting the top of the plot at about x=142 at $0.40. Series 'Vera Rubin NVL72' (green) stays near $0.00 until about x=190, then rises, exiting the top at about x=254 at $0.40. A green dashed vertical arrow at x=150 points down from the top of the plot to the baseline, labelled '45X Lower' in green. No legend box; series are identified by direct labels next to the curves. No values are printed on the curves; the 30X and 45X labels are the only printed values.

### Slide 11

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A single trend chart of IT capex share across top cloud providers is paired with a clear statement of revenue opportunity per gigawatt, so the chart carries the argument directly.
- Review: The deck has a consistent black and NVIDIA green identity, action-style titles, and charts that carry real arguments, notably the paired curves on slide 10, the per-GW bars on slide 11 and the indexed networking chart on slide 17.
- What would change the score: I would rebuild slide 28 as a visual summary tied to the three growth pillars, and cut the intro paragraphs above the charts on slides 11 and 15 to one line each.

**Text**

NVIDIA Expanding AI Market While
Expanding Revenue Opportunity Per GW
NVIDIA continues to increase its share within the overall AI infrastructure market. NVIDIA’s per gigawatt revenue opportunity – based on its reference design – has expanded
from $18 billion during the Hopper generation to $25 billion with Blackwell and now $40 billion with Rubin. The growth in NVIDIA’s SAM (served available market) has been driven
by advancements in the GPU as well as a broadening in the company’s portfolio – which now includes stand-alone CPU racks for agentic AI, LPU racks for low-latency,
large-context inference, and scale-in networking for secure, performant AI factories. This is all a reflection of NVIDIA’s strategy in becoming a full-stack AI factory platform.

**Layout**

Two equal bordered panels sit side by side in the lower half, the left panel holding a line chart and the right panel a bar chart, with a centred two-line title across the top and a centred four-line grey paragraph between title and panels. The NVIDIA logo sits bottom right; the slide has no page number or footer text, and margins are roughly 5% on the left and right sides.

**Visuals**

Two charts. Left: a line chart titled NVIDIA’s IT Capex Share Across the Top 5 CSPs with subtitle (Trailing 12-Month Basis); y-axis 0% to 50% in 5% steps, no gridlines, x-axis categories 4Q22, 1Q23, 2Q23, 3Q23, 4Q23, 1Q24, 2Q24, 3Q24, 4Q24, 1Q25, 2Q25, 3Q25, 4Q25, 1Q26, 2Q26 (solid) then CY27E (dashed projection), labels rotated about 45 degrees. One green (#76B900) series with labelled values: 4Q22 11%, 1Q23 11%, 2Q23 15%, 3Q23 20%, 4Q23 29%, 1Q24 35%, 2Q24 35%, 3Q24 36%, 4Q24 36%, 1Q25 35%, 2Q25 37%, 3Q25 39%, 4Q25 39%, 1Q26 40%, 2Q26 38%; dashed extension to CY27E 44%. Data labels in small white text with thin leader ticks; no legend; no highlighted points. Right: a column chart titled NVIDIA Increasing Platform Content Expands / Revenue Opportunity Per GW with unit line Billions ($); y-axis $0 to $60 in $10 steps; categories Hopper, Blackwell, Rubin, Feynman; no data labels on bars. Hopper bar about $18 (from text), Blackwell $25 (from text), Rubin $40 (from text), Feynman about $60 by axis reading (no value printed). Bars are solid #76B900 except Feynman, which is a vertical gradient from near-black olive at top to #76B900 at bottom. No legend. Logo: NVIDIA eye mark in green with white NVIDIA wordmark, bottom right.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 across the entire slide. Pure black background with white titles, grey body paragraph, white chart text and NVIDIA green accents. Typeface is NVIDIA Sans (a geometric humanist sans-serif): Medium weight for title and panel headings, Regular for body and axis labels. Title is about 4.9% of slide height (cap height about 3.5%), body paragraph about 2.0%, panel headings about 2.0%, axis labels about 1.7%, data labels about 1.2%. Title colour #FFFFFF, body colour #A1A1A1, accent #76B900. Panels have a thin grey outline about #4D4D4D, roughly 0.75 pt, with small corner radius, no fill and no shadow. Line is solid green about 3 pt with rounded joins; projection segment dashed green. Bars are flat with no corner radius and no shadow.

**Layout**

A single centred title sits across the top of the slide at about 10.5% from the top. Below it, four server node units are placed in a horizontal row across the width, each with a centred two-line heading and a grey descriptor above it. Their heights step up from left to right, with the Vera DPX label highest. The units are arranged in a loose row with no visible grid, rules, footer or slide number, and the margins are generous on all sides.

**Visuals**

Four photographic renderings of data centre server units, shown at three-quarter angles on a black reflective surface with soft mirror reflections beneath each unit. From left to right: (1) Vera Rubin NVL72 Compute Node, a flat tray-style board with gold-edged handles, several gold-lidded processor modules with green and blue centres, a central black heat spine and a dark board with small connectors; (2) Vera MGX Compute Node, a tray with six visible dark processor packages with blue and green centres, four small gold-framed modules along the bottom edge, and a silver-grey lower panel with gold handles; (3) Vera CMX Storage Node, an open chassis with a black interior board, two large processor packages with green centres, several smaller modules and connectors, and a silver lower panel with gold-coloured vents; (4) Vera DPX Storage Node, a taller rack-style chassis with gold-coloured heatsink fins on top, a dark circuit board with a row of connectors, and a gold-toned front grille with vent panels. Lighting is a single soft key light from above, producing highlights on the metal edges. Dominant colours are black, gold, silver-grey and small green and blue accents. Each unit is shown whole, not cropped, and is not boxed; the photograph covers the full slide.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 background, with the four units standing on a dark reflective surface.

### Slide 13

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: many slides depend on small gray body text and tiny labels (slides 4, 8, 13, 18, 21, 22), the financial tables on slide 9 are unreadable at presentation size, and slide 2 is a dense disclaimer wall.
- Suggested repair: Enlarge or simplify the small body text and diagram labels on slides 4, 8, 13, 18 and 21, and replace the screenshot-style tables on slide 9 with a few legible headline figures.

**Text**

Announcing NVIDIA Open Agent Security Platform
An Open Reference Design with NVIDIA OpenShell and NVIDIA Sentry
OpenShell is a policy-controlled runtime between an agent and the enterprise systems it can affect – data, files, credentials, tools, APIs, models, and networks.
OpenShell addresses the agent security problem: 1) Prevents uncontrolled access to enterprise data, 2) Limits credential exposure and misuse, 3) Controls agent tool use and code execution, 4) Applies default-deny network egress where appropriate, 5) Helps contain prompt injection, malicious retrieved content, and harmful tool calls, and 6) Creates an auditable runtime boundary for long-running autonomous workflows.
Secure Runtime Boundary
Governs and Mediates All Actions, Enforces Policy at Runtime
In-Silicon Out-of-Band Monitor
Agent Reasoning Inspection and Tamper-Proof Telemetry
NVIDIA OpenShell
Gateway
Supervisor
Policy Prover
Agent Harness
Agent Sandbox
NVIDIA Sentry
Hardware-Based Enforcement
Continuous Agent Monitoring and Threat Detection
Trusted Telemetry and Detection Pipeline
Millisecond-Scale Containment and Quarantine
Vera CPU
BlueField-4
Prompt / Tools / Data / Model / Policies
Prompt
Tools
Data
Model
Vera MGX Compute Node
Secure Agent Processing

**Layout**

Full-bleed black slide with a centred three-part composition: a title block across the top (main title, subtitle, and two paragraphs of grey body text centred), then three columns below. The left column holds the green-outlined NVIDIA OpenShell panel with a lock icon at its top-left corner; the centre column holds the tall Vera MGX compute node rack image with its caption beneath; the right column holds the green-outlined NVIDIA Sentry panel with a lock icon at its top-right corner and four bottom tiles beneath it. Each column has a centred heading and subheading above its panel. Margins are about 6 to 7 percent on the left and right. The NVIDIA logo sits bottom right. There is no visible slide number or footer.

**Visuals**

1) Photograph/illustration: a tall, dark, vertical compute-node rack centred horizontally at about x 40 to 60 percent and spanning roughly y 32 to 96 percent. It shows stacked black chassis with heatsink fins, four rows of square chips with coloured blue and pink-purple glowing dies on the upper and middle sections, two gold-brown chips near the bottom, a silver metal cover plate at the base, and gold-tan handles at the foot. Lighting is dim and directional with the chips lit. Dominant colours are black, dark grey, silver and gold-tan with small blue and magenta accents. The image is unboxed and sits directly on the black background; it is not cropped. Text labels Vera CPU and BlueField-4 sit on small dark boxes with thin white outlines over the chassis.
2) Diagram: a green (#76B900) double-headed arrow runs horizontally from the OpenShell panel to the rack at about y 50 percent, with arrowheads at both ends and a thin line. A green vertical double-headed arrow runs from the upper rack chips down to the bottom chips at about x 44 percent, with arrowheads at both ends. A green horizontal arrow at about y 76 percent runs from the rack's lower chips to the Sentry panel, with an arrowhead pointing right into the panel. Grey stacked labels Prompt, Tools, Data, Model, Policies sit between the rack and the Sentry panel at about x 60 percent.
3) Shapes: NVIDIA OpenShell and NVIDIA Sentry are rounded-corner rectangles with a 2 px green (#76B900) outline and no fill. Inside OpenShell are three dark grey tiles (Gateway, Supervisor, Policy Prover) and one larger dark grey tile (Agent Harness) inside a white dashed rounded rectangle labelled Agent Sandbox. Inside Sentry are four dark grey tiles in a vertical stack. Below Sentry are four dark grey tiles in a row, each linked by a grey dashed double-headed arrow to the Sentry panel.
4) Icons: two green outline padlocks, one at the top-left corner of the OpenShell panel and one at the top-right corner of the Sentry panel.
5) Logo: the NVIDIA logo in green and white at bottom right.

Background: Solid black #000000 full-bleed background. Black (#000000) background with no gradient. ### Slide 14

**Text**

NVIDIA Vera Delivers / Maximum Single-Threaded / Performance at Scale / NVIDIA’s data center CPU journey began in 2021 with the / introduction of the Grace CPU. / Today, we are in full production of our next-generation Vera CPU. / As a stand-alone product, Vera expands NVIDIA’s SAM even further. / CPUs have historically traded speed against volume: how fast one / task finishes versus how many run at once. Pre-cloud CPUs chose / speed, while cloud CPUs chose volume (more but slower cores). / Agentic AI needs both, and Vera is the first data center CPU / purpose-built for these workloads. / High / NEW / Pre-Cloud CPU / Frequency-Optimized / Max Single-Threaded / CPU at Scale / Single-Threaded / Core Performance / Low / Cloud CPU / Scale Out / Low / High / Throughput

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a black slide: a left text column (title and four body paragraphs) starts at about 5% from the left edge and occupies roughly the top-left to middle-left area, and a right-hand 2x2 matrix chart spans about 58% to 93% of the slide width. The title sits top-left, left-aligned at about 22% of slide height, with the body text stacked beneath it; the matrix is vertically centred on the right with its axis labels outside its left and bottom edges. The NVIDIA logo sits bottom-right at about 93% x and 94% y, with no page number or footer text visible.

**Visuals**

Diagram: a 2x2 quadrant matrix drawn with thin light-grey (#757575) lines, outer frame about 58.3% to 92.6% of slide width and 18.0% to 75.1% of slide height, with a vertical divider at about 75.4% of width and a horizontal divider at about 46.3% of height. Y-axis labelled High (top) and Low (bottom) with the green rotated-free label Single-Threaded Core Performance placed between them on the left; X-axis labelled Low (left column) and High (right column) beneath the frame, with the green label Throughput centred below. Top-left quadrant: small grey filled dot (#757575, about 1% diameter) above the grey label Pre-Cloud CPU and italic grey Frequency-Optimized. Top-right quadrant: highlighted box with a bright green (#76B900) border about 0.3% wide and a dark green tint fill (#598B00 at 15% opacity over black, appearing about #0E1A00); small bright green filled dot (#76B900) above green label NEW, then white label Max Single-Threaded / CPU at Scale. Bottom-right quadrant: small grey filled dot (#757575) above grey label Cloud CPU and italic grey Scale Out. Bottom-left quadrant is empty. No photographs, icons, or charts beyond this matrix; the only logo is the NVIDIA wordmark with its eye symbol in green (#76B900) and white text at bottom right.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 across the entire slide. Pure black background (#000000) with no gradient or texture. Title text is white (#FFFFFF) NVIDIASans Medium, a clean geometric humanist sans-serif, approximately 4.9% of slide height per line with tight leading (about 5.3% line pitch), left-aligned. Body text is light grey (#A1A1A1) NVIDIASans Regular sans-serif at about 2.0% of slide height with generous line spacing (about 2.6% pitch between lines within a paragraph, paragraphs separated by about 1.0% extra space). Accent colour is NVIDIA green #76B900 used for the green axis label, NEW tag, and highlight border and dot. Matrix labels are white (#FFFFFF) for High/Low axis ends and quadrant names in the highlighted box, grey (#757575) for the non-highlighted quadrant labels, and green (#76B900) for the axis titles. Lines are thin grey strokes (#757575, roughly 0.1% of slide height) forming a square-cornered grid with no radius and no shadows; the highlighted quadrant has a green (#76B900) outline about 0.3% of slide height wide with square corners.

**Text**

NVIDIA Vera Delivers Over 2X More Performance
and Memory Bandwidth for a 100 MW CPU Data Center

**Layout**

Single centred column on a full-black slide: a two-line centred title near the top, a three-line centred grey subtitle beneath it, and one bordered chart panel centred in the lower-middle area. The NVIDIA logo sits in the bottom-right corner. No footer text or slide number is visible. Margins are about 10 percent on the left and right sides.

**Visuals**

Grouped bar chart titled 100 MW CPU Data Center, set inside a thin-outlined rounded rectangle panel. Legend above the plot: grey square followed by x86 CPU 128C, then green square followed by NVIDIA Vera 88C. Y axis runs from 0.0X to 2.5X in steps of 0.5X, labelled with X-suffixed values and no axis title. X axis categories are CPUs (k), Throughput (SPECrate2026_int_base) and Memory Bandwidth (PB/s). Series values: CPUs (k) x86 140 at 1.0X and Vera 180 at 1.2X; Throughput x86 3,600 at 1.0X and Vera 7,600 at 2.1X; Memory Bandwidth x86 90 at 1.0X and Vera 210 at 2.4X. Raw values are printed inside the bars in black bold or medium type, and multiplier labels sit above each bar in white. Grey bars are #989898 and green bars are #76B900, with a thin grey baseline. No highlighted points beyond the green series.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 across the whole slide. Pure black background (#000000) with white (#FFFFFF) titles and chart labels and mid-grey (#A1A1A1) subtitle body text. Accent green #76B900 for the Vera series and logo. Typeface is NVIDIASans, a geometric humanist sans-serif, Medium for title and bold-ish labels, Regular for body. Title cap height is roughly 4.9 percent of slide height (about 5 percent font size), subtitle about 2.0 percent. Chart panel has a 1 px grey (#A1A1A1-like) outline with rounded corners of about 1 percent of slide height, no fill and no shadow. Bars are flat with no gradients or shadows.

### Slide 16

**Text**

Groq 3 LPX Co-Designed with Vera Rubin / Delivers Leadership Low-Latency Inference Performance / NVIDIA is in full production of Groq 3 LPX, the company’s first rack-scale LPU system. Together with the Vera Rubin NVL72 system, LPX expands the frontier / addressable by NVIDIA’s full-stack computing platform – specifically, the low-latency inference segment of the market. Groq 3 LPX is already setting records, / demonstrating nearly 4x the number of tokens per second against the next best alternative on Artificial Analysis’ Gemma 4 31B benchmark. / Throughput (TPS/MW) / Vera Rubin NVL72 / Blackwell / NVL72 / Hopper / NVL8 / LPX / Lowest Latency / Inference / User Interactivity (TPS / User) / Output Speed: Gemma 4 31B (Reasoning) / Output speed: o200k_base tokens per second · 100,000 input tokens / 3,431 / 4X Faster / 870 / 188 / 151 / 101 / 48 / 35 / 34 / 31 / 29 / NVIDIA Groq 3 LPX / Private, Pre-Release / Endpoint / Live Serverless Data / Public API Endpoints / Source: Artificial Analysis / NVIDIA logo

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a black field: a centred two-line title sits at the top, a centred three-line grey subtitle paragraph sits beneath it, and two equal-height outlined panels fill the lower two-thirds, the left panel (line chart) about 35% of the width and the right panel (bar chart) about 54% of the width, with a gutter of about 2% between them. Panel titles are centred at the top of each panel; the source line sits bottom-right inside the right panel; the NVIDIA logo sits bottom-right of the slide. There is no slide number or footer text.

**Visuals**

Two charts. (1) Left: a line-style throughput diagram titled 'Throughput (TPS/MW)' with x-axis 'User Interactivity (TPS / User)' and y-axis unlabelled, drawn as arrowed grey axes meeting at the bottom-left corner. Three curves: a green curve labelled 'Vera Rubin NVL72' starts high at the left axis, stays flat, then falls steeply to the baseline near x 23.5%; a white curve labelled 'Blackwell NVL72' starts at mid-height and falls to the baseline near x 13.4%; a short grey curve labelled 'Hopper NVL8' sits at the origin. A flat green segment labelled 'LPX' / 'Lowest Latency Inference' begins at the foot of the Vera Rubin drop and runs right and slightly down to the x-axis end. Two dashed green arrows: one diagonal from the Blackwell curve up toward the Vera Rubin curve, one horizontal from the LPX start pointing right. No values are printed on the axes. (2) Right: vertical bar chart titled 'Output Speed: Gemma 4 31B (Reasoning)' with subtitle 'Output speed: o200k_base tokens per second · 100,000 input tokens'. Y-axis is unlabelled with no printed scale; x-axis has no tick labels. Ten bars with printed values: NVIDIA Groq 3 LPX 3,431 (green, the highlighted bar, the tallest); 870; 188; 151; 101; 48; 35; 34; 31; 29 (grey). Values for the grey bars after 870 are printed; the bar heights for those are about proportional to value on a linear scale with 3,431 as full height. A green horizontal reference line runs from the top of the 3,431 bar to the right at the level of its top. A grey dashed double-headed vertical arrow runs from that reference line down to the top of the 870 bar, labelled '4X Faster' in green. Bar group labels: 'NVIDIA Groq 3 LPX' under the green bar, 'Private, Pre-Release / Endpoint' bold under it; 'Live Serverless Data / Public API Endpoints' under the grey bars. No legend.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 covering the whole slide. Pure black background (#000000) with white (#FFFFFF) titles and chart labels, grey (#A1A1A1) subtitle body text, mid-grey (#757575) source line, light grey (#D9D9D9) secondary label, NVIDIA green (#76B900) for accents, the highlighted bar, the 4X Faster label, the Vera Rubin and LPX labels and curves. Typeface is NVIDIA Sans (a humanist sans-serif), Medium weight for titles and labels, Regular weight for the subtitle and source line. Title cap height about 4.9% of slide height (font size 4.94% of height); subtitle about 2.0% of height; chart labels about 1.2 to 1.5%. Panel outlines are thin grey strokes (about #6B6B6B, roughly 1px) with small corner radii (about 1% of slide height). Grey bars are #757575, flat with no shadow or gradient; axes are thin grey (#757575) lines with small arrowheads. No shadows or gradients.

### Slide 17

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: An indexed growth chart against the total market is labelled with a CAGR comparison, turning a single claim into a visible contrast.
- Review: The deck has a consistent black and NVIDIA green identity, action-style titles, and charts that carry real arguments, notably the paired curves on slide 10, the per-GW bars on slide 11 and the indexed networking chart on slide 17.
- Worth keeping: Keep the paired comparison charts on slide 10, the green-against-gray emphasis with annotated multiples, and the single-chart-with-numbered-list layout on slide 17.

**Text**

NVIDIA is the Largest and
Fastest Growing AI Networking
Company in the World
NVIDIA is the only company that offers five networking technologies:
1. Scale-Up (NVLink)
2. Scale-Out (InfiniBand, Spectrum-X Ethernet)
3. Scale-Across (Spectrum-XGS Ethernet)
4. Scale-In (BlueField-4 DPU and DOCA over Spectrum-X Ethernet)
5. Context Scale (Vera BlueField-4 Storage Processor)
Since the acquisition of Mellanox in 2020, NVIDIA's Networking revenue
has grown at a CAGR of 70%, materially outperforming industry peers.
Today, NVIDIA is the largest and fastest growing AI Networking company
in the world.

**Layout**

Two-column layout on a black field. The left column (text block, x about 5% to 47%) is vertically centred, with the title at the top of that block and the body text below it, left-aligned to a shared margin at about 5.2% of slide width. The right column (x about 50.7% to 95.1%) holds a row of five product tiles across the top (y about 14.9% to 29.6%) above a single bordered chart panel (y about 31% to 85.8%). The NVIDIA logo sits bottom right at about x 92.9%, y 94.2%. No slide number or footer text is visible.

**Visuals**

Five product photographs in a row, each in a thin-bordered rounded rectangle, with a green label above and a small white caption below. (1) Scale-Up, labelled NVLink: a dark grey vertical NVLink switch/compute board with a heatsink and connectors, shown upright, centred on black, small framing with black surround. (2) Scale-Out, labelled Spectrum-X Ethernet: a dark metal networking switch chassis in a three-quarter angled view with a visible fan grille and a round top vent, lit with warm light, filling the tile and cropped at the edges. (3) Scale-Across, labelled Spectrum-XGS Ethernet: a diagram-style illustration on black of four small grey switch boxes arranged around a central box, connected by thin white lines, with small labels reading Scale-Up, Scale-Across and Scale-Out in tiny text. (4) Scale-In, labelled BlueField-4 DPU: a dark green-black circuit board with a central square chip, heatsinks and connectors, shown flat, black background. (5) Context Scale, labelled Vera BlueField-4 Storage Processor: a dark grey rack server chassis viewed from the front-top angle with an open lid, showing a green-lit board inside and rows of drive bays. Below the tiles, one bordered chart: a line chart titled Networking Revenue (Indexed CY2020 = 100). Legend above plot, centred: green line NVIDIA, grey line Total Market. Y axis: 0 to 1,600 in steps of 200, unitless index, no gridlines, tick labels at left. X axis: 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025 along a thin grey baseline at 0. Series NVIDIA (green #76B900, thick line, labelled values): 2020 100, 2021 110, 2022 169, 2023 392, 2024 594, 2025 1435. Series Total Market (grey #A1A1A1, labelled values): 2020 100, 2021 109, 2022 139, 2023 180, 2024 218, 2025 347. Labels are printed at each point; the 2025 NVIDIA point is highlighted in green with its label at the top. Inside the plot, at left-centre, a text callout reads CAGR (CY2020-2025) with NVIDIA – 70% and Market – 28% in white. Chart has no visible axis titles. Also the NVIDIA logo: green eye-shaped mark with the wordmark NVIDIA in white, bottom right.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 covering the whole slide. Black background #000000. Titles white #FFFFFF in NVIDIASans Medium, a clean geometric humanist sans-serif, sentence-style capitalisation as printed. Title size about 4.9% of slide height with tight line spacing (about 1.1). Body text grey #A1A1A1 in NVIDIASans Regular, about 2.0% of slide height, with generous line spacing. Accent colour NVIDIA green #76B900 for list numbers, tile labels, chart NVIDIA series and its data labels. Tile captions are white #FFFFFF at about 1.0% of slide height. Chart panel and tile frames are outlined with thin grey strokes (about #5A5A5A, roughly 1 px) with small rounded corners (radius about 1% of slide width), no fill, no shadows, no gradients. Numbered list uses green numerals with a hanging indent and no bullets. Chart text in white or grey, chart data labels in green or grey, Medium weight for the callout and labels.

### Slide 18

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: many slides depend on small gray body text and tiny labels (slides 4, 8, 13, 18, 21, 22), the financial tables on slide 9 are unreadable at presentation size, and slide 2 is a dense disclaimer wall.
- Suggested repair: Enlarge or simplify the small body text and diagram labels on slides 4, 8, 13, 18 and 21, and replace the screenshot-style tables on slide 9 with a few legible headline figures.

**Text**

NVLink Fusion Expands NVIDIA Opportunities
NVIDIA NVLink Fusion offers customers the option to build semi-custom AI factories by integrating their custom XPUs or CPUs with NVIDIA’s proven rack architecture.
And to accelerate adoption, NVIDIA is partnering with various CPU, custom silicon, EDA and optical interconnect suppliers.
Customer reception has been strong with AWS, Intel, RIKEN, and others already committed to adopting NVLink scale-up technology.
NVLink Fusion opportunity to approach $250 billion by the end of the decade.
NVLink Connectors
XPU
XPU Package
NVIDIA NVLink 6
Switch
NVIDIA Vera or
Custom CPU
NVIDIA NVLink
Cable Cartridge
NVIDIA Grace
BlueField
NVIDIA ConnectX-9
Compute Tray x18
NVIDIA NVLink Switch Tray x9
NVIDIA NVLink Spine x1
NVIDIA MGX Rack
Cableless, Hoseless, Modular Design
3.6 TB/s per XPU All-to-All Bandwidth
Connects 72 XPUs in a Single Domain
Third-Generation Design
45C Inlet Temp
3X Lower Latency
100% Liquid Cooled
Built-In Factory Resiliency

**Layout**

Centred single-column header with the title at the top and four centred body lines beneath it, followed by a horizontal row of one XPU chip and four product columns (Compute Tray, NVLink Switch Tray, NVLink Spine, MGX Rack), each with a centred bold caption and small centred sub-lines, joined left to right by two white arrows. Left margin of the diagram row is about 6.7% and right margin about 17% for the rack; the NVIDIA logo sits bottom right. No footer text and no slide number.

**Visuals**

Five visuals on a black field. (1) XPU: a square dark-grey (#262626) chip package about 8.5% by 15.5% of the slide with tan (#C8B98A) contact pads along its top, bottom, left and right edges, and a blue (#0074DF, 90% opacity) die with a grid of lighter blue cells labelled XPU in white at its centre. (2) White arrow pointing right from the XPU to the Compute Tray. (3) Photograph of a Compute Tray x18: a black-background, backlit, line-drawn style top view of a server tray with four glowing XPU packages in a row at the top, two smaller glowing Vera or custom CPU modules below them, a single glowing Grace BlueField module lower centre and two small ConnectX-9 modules at the bottom; lit with amber and white highlights, full frame, boxed to about 10.9% by 34.1%, dominant colours black, gold and amber. Leader lines and labels: NVLink Connectors (white, from the top edge of the tray diagonally up-right to the label), XPU Package (horizontal line to the upper-row packages), NVIDIA Vera or Custom CPU (horizontal line to a CPU module), NVIDIA Grace BlueField (horizontal line to the Grace module), NVIDIA ConnectX-9 (horizontal line to the bottom modules). (4) Photograph of an NVIDIA NVLink Switch Tray x9: a black-background, backlit line-drawn view of a server tray with four glowing rectangular NVLink 6 switch chips arranged in a V shape at the upper centre, with cabling and board detail below, boxed to about 10.9% by 34.0%, dominant colours black, gold and amber. Leader lines: NVIDIA NVLink 6 Switch (horizontal white line to the right-hand switch chips) and NVLink Connectors (diagonal white line from the top connector area of this tray to the label above). (5) Photograph of an NVIDIA NVLink Spine x1: four tall black vertical cable-cartridge modules standing side by side, each with a column of small glowing gold/amber contact points down its inner edge, on a black background, boxed to about 11.3% by 28.7%; a white leader line from the NVIDIA NVLink Cable Cartridge label points to the left-hand module. (6) White arrow pointing right from the Spine to the MGX Rack. (7) Photograph of an NVIDIA MGX Rack: a tall black enclosure with a thin grey frame, a brushed-gold lit band of horizontal rails across its middle, dark top and base, shown front-on and centred, boxed to about 6.6% by 44.9%, dominant colours black and gold. (8) NVIDIA logo bottom right in green and white. Nothing is cropped, full-bleed or tinted except the photographs which sit on the black background.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 covering the whole slide. Black (#000000) background throughout with no gradient or texture. Title in white (#FFFFFF) NVIDIA Sans Medium (a geometric grotesque sans-serif), about 4.9% of slide height, centred. Body paragraphs in mid-grey (#A1A1A1) NVIDIA Sans Regular, about 2.0% of slide height, centred, with generous line spacing. Sub-captions and labels in white (#FFFFFF) NVIDIA Sans Regular about 1.5% (labels) and 1.2% (sub-lines); column headings in white NVIDIA Sans Medium about 1.7%. Accent colours: XPU die blue #0074DF at 90% opacity, chip frame #262626, tan pads #C8B98A, NVIDIA logo green #76B900. Leader lines and arrows are thin white strokes (about 1 px) with no shadows, no rounded corners and no gradients.

**Text**

NVIDIA CUDA Turns Programmability into Ecosystem Scale
NVIDIA CUDA defines NVIDIA’s strategy and powers the flywheel. Developers drive new applications. New applications create new ecosystems.
New ecosystems grow the installed base. And a larger installed base invites more developers.
NVIDIA compute accelerates every workload. AI and non-AI. All fields of science.
NVIDIA continuously updates its software, driving down the cost of computing, and improving the installed base’s productivity.
Built on CUDA, NVIDIA compute is versatile, fungible, durable, and therefore investable.

**Layout**

Single black slide with a centred title at the top and a centred four-paragraph subtitle block beneath it. The lower half is split into two zones: a circular flywheel diagram on the left and a 2×2 grid of bubble charts on the right, each chart with its label at left, a thin baseline, and two year columns (2021 and 2026 Q2). The NVIDIA logo sits in the bottom-right corner. No footer or slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

Left: a circular flywheel diagram with four white hand-drawn curved arrows arranged clockwise in a ring around a green handwritten label. The ring's four nodes are NEW APPLICATIONS (top), DEVELOPERS (right), INSTALLED BASE (bottom) and ECOSYSTEMS (left); the arrows run ECOSYSTEMS to NEW APPLICATIONS (arrowhead at the top), NEW APPLICATIONS to DEVELOPERS (arrowhead at the upper right), DEVELOPERS to INSTALLED BASE (arrowhead at the lower right), and INSTALLED BASE to ECOSYSTEMS (arrowhead at the lower left). The centre reads 20 YEARS / CUDA / FLYWHEEL in #76B900 handwritten capitals. Right: four bubble charts, each with two solid #76B900 circles scaled by value. Developers: 3M (2021, small circle) and 11M (2026 Q2, large circle), circle sizes about proportional to value. AI Startups: 9K (2021) and 50K (2026 Q2). CUDA Downloads: 32M (2021, smallest circle) and 1.2B (2026 Q2, largest circle). GPU-Accelerated Applications: 2K (2021) and 9K (2026 Q2). Each chart has a thin grey baseline with the category label to its left. Values are printed above the circles in bold white; no axes or legend. Only the 2026 Q2 values are printed larger than the 2021 values in the same chart; the circle areas are not labelled as estimates.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 covering the whole slide. Black background (#000000) with white titles (#FFFFFF), grey body text (#A1A1A1) and bright green accents (#76B900). Typeface is NVIDIA Sans (a geometric grotesque sans-serif) in Medium for the title, Regular for body and labels, and Bold for the bubble values. Title is about 4.9% of slide height; subtitle and chart labels about 2.0%; bubble values and year labels about 1.7%. Lines are thin grey hairlines about 0.1% high. Bubbles are flat solid green circles with no outline, shadow or gradient. Arrows are white with rough hand-drawn edges.

### Slide 20

**Text**

NVIDIA CUDA-X Accelerates
Apps and Reduces the Cost of Computing
CUDA-X libraries accelerate apps, enabling them to run orders of magnitude faster, more efficiently, and at lower cost than on CPUs. Over time the introduction of new
libraries expands our platform's addressable set of applications and use cases, unlocking new markets and increasing our long-term growth opportunity.
With ~9,000 apps, AI models, and libraries running on CUDA, NVIDIA addresses the largest market opportunity of any computing platform.
cuLitho
Computational Lithography
cuDSS
cuSPARSE
cuFFT

**Layout**

Centered header block across the top: two-line title, then two centred paragraphs of grey body text, with about 5 percent top margin and no footer or slide number. Below, thirteen tall rounded image cards sit in a single horizontal row spanning the slide width, stepping slightly upward and shrinking from left to right, each with a centred label block (name in tan, then a grey descriptor) beneath it.

**Visuals**

Thirteen rectangular photographic or rendered cards in one row, left to right: (1) cuLitho: tall dark grey block structure with blue and gold circuit-like patterning, lit by bright blue and gold light, on a blue-black ground. (2) cuDSS: a black pipe seen in cross-section with a rainbow-coloured CFD flow stream on a blue sky background. (3) cuOpt: a purple 3D surface plot with a white line chart drawn across it, on a dark dotted grid. (4) Warp: a grey box with a white wall and coloured streamlines in red, green and blue on a dark grey floor with a grey sphere. (5) cuDF and cuML: black background with a grid and scattered coloured particle clouds in yellow, orange, cyan, pink and purple. (6) Megatron Dynamo NIXL cuDNN CUTLASS: dark teal background with a white-outlined network diagram of stacked blocks linked by lines. (7) cuEquivariance cuTensor: a ball-and-stick molecule model on black with red, blue, cyan, white and yellow atoms. (8) cuQuantum CUDA-Q: a gold dilution refrigerator cryostat with copper plates, on a dark background. (9) Earth-2: a view of Earth from space showing blue ocean, brown and green land and white clouds. (10) MONAI: colourful 3D segmented organ shapes in green, red, blue and yellow on a grey background. (11) Parabricks: a lavender double helix of DNA on a dark purple-black background. (12) Aerial Sionna: an aerial view of rooftops and streets with bright green ray paths on a grey-white city. (13) cuPyNumeric: a spiral galaxy with a purple and pink core on a deep blue starfield. Each card is a rounded-corner rectangle with a thin pale gold edge and a soft drop shadow. Below the cards is a faint, dark mirrored reflection of the row fading to black.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000. Pure black background (#000000) with a sans-serif NVIDIA house typeface (NVIDIA Sans). Title is white (#FFFFFF), medium weight, roughly 4.9 percent of slide height. Body paragraphs are mid grey (#A1A1A1), regular weight, roughly 2 percent of slide height. Card labels use a tan-gold (#C8B88A) medium weight for names and a light grey (#D0D0D0) regular weight for descriptors at about 1.5 percent. Cards have rounded corners of about 1 percent of slide width, thin pale gold outlines and soft dark shadows. No gradients except the fading reflection at the bottom.

### Slide 21

**Review notes**

- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: many slides depend on small gray body text and tiny labels (slides 4, 8, 13, 18, 21, 22), the financial tables on slide 9 are unreadable at presentation size, and slide 2 is a dense disclaimer wall.
- Suggested repair: Enlarge or simplify the small body text and diagram labels on slides 4, 8, 13, 18 and 21, and replace the screenshot-style tables on slide 9 with a few legible headline figures.

**Text**

NVIDIA AI Factories Are Productive, Durable, and Fungible / Productive / Profitable Tokens / Payback well inside / depreciation window / $100M / $75M / $30M / $10M / Cost / Open Models / Frontier Labs / ISP Tokens / Revenue $M/MW/year / Cost: $60M/MW build · 6-yr depreciation / Durable / CUDA-Driven Installed-Base Enhancement / Earns after full depreciation and / improves over time through software / V100 / DGX-1 / 10 yrs in service / A100 / 6 yrs old · 9 yrs sold / NVL72 / 1 yr old · 7 yrs sold / Fungible / One CUDA Architecture – Global Ecosystem / One platform, / many offtakers, any workload / AI Labs / Every Closed / and Open Model / Financial Services / Jane Street, / HRT, JPM, GS / Industrial / LG, Cadence / Pharma / Roche, Lilly / Automotive / Hyundai, BMW

**Layout**

Three equal-height outlined panels sit side by side in three columns across the lower two-thirds of the slide, each with a green column heading and white subheading above it. The title is centred across the top at about 10.5% from the top edge, and the NVIDIA logo sits bottom right. Left and right margins are about 5%, and the panels are separated by gaps of about 1.5%.

**Visuals**

Three panels. Left panel: vertical bar chart with title 'Payback well inside depreciation window' and a y-axis that is not drawn. Bars from left to right: Cost $10M (grey #757575), Open Models $30M (dark green about #3E6600), Frontier Labs $75M (green #5E9000, #76B900 at 80 percent opacity), ISP Tokens $100M (lime #B8FF3C). Values are printed above each bar. Bars sit on a thin grey baseline at about 76.5% of slide height; axis labels are printed under each bar. Footnote under the chart gives the units and assumptions. The chart has no legend and no gridlines. ISP Tokens is the highlighted bar. Middle panel: three horizontal stacked bars on a shared 0 to full-width scale with no axis. V100 / DGX-1: one full-width bar in #76B900 labelled 10 yrs in service. A100: green segment from about 38.1% to 52.4% of slide width, dark green #3E6600 segment to about 59.5%, grey #757575 segment to about 62.0%, labelled 6 yrs old · 9 yrs sold. NVL72: green segment 38.1% to about 40.5%, dark green to about 54.8%, grey to about 62.0%, labelled 1 yr old · 7 yrs sold. Each bar has a dark track #2A2E31 behind it. Right panel: six line icons in lime green #76B900 with bold white labels. AI Labs icon shows three people figures above a stacked group. Financial Services icon shows a rising line chart over three bars with a dollar coin. Industrial icon shows a factory with chimneys. Pharma icon shows a medicine bottle with a cross and a capsule. Automotive icon shows a side-view car, with a thin green leader line crossing it from the Financial Services label area. All icons are outline style with about 0.2% stroke weight.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 covering the whole slide. Pure black background. Title in white #FFFFFF, subtitles in white #FFFFFF, column headings in NVIDIA green #76B900, and body and labels in white #FFFFFF or grey #989898 and #A1A1A1. Accents are #76B900, dark green #3E6600, lime #B8FF3C and grey #757575. Typeface is NVIDIASans, a clean geometric sans-serif, in Medium for the title and headings, Regular for body text and Bold for category labels. Title is about 4.9% of slide height, column headings about 2.5%, subheads and panel headlines about 2.2%, and body and labels about 1.5% to 2.0%. Panels have 1px grey #5A5A5A rounded outlines with corner radii of about 0.5% of slide height and no fill or shadow.

### Slide 22

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A residual value curve by GPU generation against an accelerated depreciation line makes an accounting argument visual and easy to follow.
- Review: Weaknesses are consequential: many slides depend on small gray body text and tiny labels (slides 4, 8, 13, 18, 21, 22), the financial tables on slide 9 are unreadable at presentation size, and slide 2 is a dense disclaimer wall.

**Text**

NVIDIA AI Infrastructure Retains Value
Beyond Accelerated Depreciation Schedules
8-GPU HGX System Cost Per GPU · Dotted Curve = Accelerated Schedule, 25% Residual at Year 2, Zero by Year 5
Residual Value, % of Purchase Price

Years Since Mass Availability
Residual value: Silicon Data, monthly averages. Purchase price basis: A100 $20,000 · H100 $35,000 · B200 $45,000.
Mass-availability dates assumed; purchase prices are third-party estimates, not Silicon Data data.

**Layout**

Centred stack on a black slide: a two-line centred title at the top, a grey centred subtitle beneath it, then one large rounded-outline chart panel spanning roughly 13% to 87% of the width and from about 23% to 90% of the height. Two-line source footer sits bottom left at about 1.7% margin and the NVIDIA logo sits bottom right; no slide number is visible.

**Visuals**

One line chart titled 'Residual Value, % of Purchase Price', with the x axis 'Years Since Mass Availability' running 0 to 8 (ticks every 1 year, unit years) and the y axis 'Residual Value, % of Purchase Price' running 0% to about 160% (labelled ticks 0%, 50%, 100%, 150%, no unit beyond %). Series: (1) Dotted white curve 'Accelerated Depreciation' from about (0 yr, 100%) falling convexly to (2 yr, 25%, labelled '25% at Year 2' with a small white dot marker) and reaching (5 yr, 0%); it is a decay curve, values at other points about: 1 yr about 50%, 3 yr about 12%, 4 yr about 6%. (2) Orange solid wavy line 'B200 158%' from about 0.8 yr at about 138% rising to a peak about 1.4 yr near 162%, ending at about 1.5 yr at 158%, labelled at its end. (3) Blue solid wavy line 'H100 58%' from about 2.5 yr at about 55% dipping to about 43% at 3.2 yr, rising to a peak about 62% at 3.7 yr and ending at 4.0 yr at 58%, labelled at its end. (4) Green solid wavy line 'A100 25%' from about 4.8 yr at about 41% declining in steps to about 27% near 6 yr and ending at about 6.3 yr at 25%, labelled at its end. No legend; series are identified by end labels. Highlighted point: the white dot at 2 yr, 25% on the dotted curve. Faint horizontal gridlines at 50%, 100% and 150%, with a slightly brighter baseline at 0%.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000. Pure black background, no gradients or textures. Title white #FFFFFF in NVIDIASans Medium, a clean geometric sans-serif, about 4.9% of slide height per line, centred. Subtitle #A1A1A1 in NVIDIASans Regular, about 2.0% of height. Chart title and axis labels white #FFFFFF or #EDEDED in NVIDIASans Regular, about 1.7% to 2.0% of height. Footer #757575 in NVIDIASans Regular, about 1.2% of height. Accent colours: orange #F5A623 for B200, blue #4DA3FF for H100, green #76B900 for A100 and the NVIDIA logo, white dotted line for the accelerated schedule. Chart panel is a thin 1px #8C8C8C rounded rectangle outline with a small corner radius of about 0.6% of slide width, no fill and no shadow. Gridlines are hairlines about #1F1F1F.

**Layout**

Single centred title across the top at about 10.5% from the top, with a three-line centred subtitle block beneath it. Below, two equal-width rounded panels sit side by side on a black field, the left panel holding a 100% stacked area chart and the right panel a line chart with a 'Source: OpenRouter' line at its lower right; the NVIDIA logo sits bottom right, with no slide number or footer.

**Visuals**

Two charts. (1) Left chart: 100% stacked area chart titled 'Open Router Token Volume - Open vs. Closed', legend 'Proprietary' (blue square #0074DF) and 'Open-Weight' (green square #76B900). Y axis 0% to 100% in 10% steps, no unit beyond percent (share of token volume). X axis monthly categories Jan-25 to Aug-26, labels rotated about 45 degrees. Proprietary series (bottom, blue) with about values: Jan-25 ~76%, Feb-25 ~79%, Mar-25 ~76%, Apr-25 ~75%, May-25 ~80%, Jun-25 ~77%, Jul-25 ~69%, Aug-25 ~62%, Sep-25 ~76%, Oct-25 ~76%, Nov-25 ~78%, Dec-25 ~70%, Jan-26 ~67%, Feb-26 ~50%, Mar-26 ~57%, Apr-26 ~61%, May-26 ~42%, Jun-26 ~32%, Jul-26 ~24%, Aug-26 ~27%. Open-Weight series (top, green) fills the remainder to 100%; no values are printed. No highlighted points. (2) Right chart: line chart titled 'Open Router Monthly Token Traffic' with subtitle '(Trillions)'. Y axis 0 to 400 in steps of 50, unit trillions (tokens). X axis labelled Jan 2025, Apr 2025, Jul 2025, Oct 2025, Jan 2026, Apr 2026, Jul 2026 at quarterly intervals, baseline grey. One green line (#76B900, about 2.5pt, no markers) with printed value labels in white above each point, in order: 2, 3, 6, 8, 10, 9, 11, 15, 21, 22, 27, 26, 32, 50, 84, 97, 123, 188, 248, 379. Rises from about 2 at the left to 379 at the right end, the steepest rise in the final months. No legend; no highlighted points. Source line 'Source: OpenRouter' in grey at lower right of the panel. (3) NVIDIA logo (green eye mark with white NVIDIA wordmark) bottom right.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 across the whole slide. Pure black #000000 background with no texture. Title in white #FFFFFF NVIDIASans Medium, sans-serif, about 4.9% of slide height, sentence case. Subtitle lines in grey #A1A1A1 NVIDIASans Regular about 2.0% of height, centred, with generous line spacing (about 4.1% pitch). Chart titles white NVIDIASans Medium about 2.0%; axis and data labels white NVIDIASans Regular about 1.5%; source line grey #757575. Accent colours: NVIDIA green #76B900 for Open-Weight and line series, blue #0074DF for Proprietary. Panels are outlined rounded rectangles with a thin grey stroke (about #5A5A5A, roughly 1 px) and no fill, corner radius about 1% of slide width, no shadow.

### Slide 24

**Text**

NVIDIA is the Largest
Contributor to Open Models
NVIDIA builds frontier open models and contributes data, software, and standards
across the AI ecosystem. We are committed to investing in and growing the open
source community to enable the ecosystem and drive accelerated growth.
NVIDIA’s investments in open models span Nemotron (Language and Agentic AI),
Cosmos (Physical AI), Isaac GR00T (Robotics), Alpamayo (Autonomous Vehicles),
and more.
On September 3rd, NVIDIA announced its intent to acquire Hugging Face.
Together, we will scale Hugging Face’s platform, strengthen its infrastructure
and expand access to AI for developers and institutions worldwide.

**Layout**

Two columns on a black field: a left text column starting at about 27.5% of slide height with the title and three body paragraphs, left-aligned at x 5.2%; a right column holding a row of five outlined cards across the top (y 18.8% to 29.4%) above a single outlined chart panel (y 31.0% to 85.8%, x 50.7% to 95.0%). No footer text or slide number; the NVIDIA logo sits bottom right and the AI World logo sits inside the chart panel bottom right.

**Visuals**

Line chart inside a rounded outlined panel. Type: multi-series cumulative line chart titled by the y-axis label 'Repos'; x-axis labelled with months Jan to Sep spanning 2025-2026 (caption '2025-2026' below axis). Y-axis ticks printed: 0, 200, 400, 600, 800, 1,000 (range 0 to about 1,000). X-axis runs Jan 2025 to Sep 2026 (21 monthly ticks). Series (values printed only as end labels; all others are estimates): NVIDIA in bright green #76B900, thick line, highlighted with a green-outlined circle containing the NVIDIA logo and label 'NVIDIA', reaching about 950 by Sep 2026; Alibaba Cloud in orange #F26B1D, ending about 510 (label circled orange with a bracket icon); Hugging Face in yellow #FFD21E, ending about 380 (circle with a yellow face icon); Meta in blue #1877F2, ending about 290 (circle with infinity icon); Microsoft in red-orange, about 245 (small logo square); IBM in light blue, about 230; Google in green, about 215 (four-colour icon); Baidu in dark red-blue, about 190 (paw icon); AMD in red, about 180 (black circle with red arrow icon); ByteDance in cyan #25F4EE, about 135 (bars icon, flat line); Z.ai in grey, about 80 (circle with Z icon). Each end label has a small circular logo outline in its series colour. No legend box; labels act as legend. Highlighted point: NVIDIA end point circled. Source line 'Source: Hugging Face' with 'Hugging Face' underlined in green. AI World logo: blue square with snowflake-like icon and 'AI WORLD' text in blue, cropped as a placed image. Three card-row icons: none (cards are text only). NVIDIA logo bottom right in green and white.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000. Entirely dark: black background #000000, white titles #FFFFFF, body text grey #A1A1A1, card titles and accent green #76B900, source label grey #757575, chart panel border grey about #5A5A5A 1px. Typeface NVIDIASans, a geometric humanist sans-serif. Title Medium weight about 4.9% of slide height (cap height about 3.4%). Body Regular about 2.0% of slide height. Card titles Medium about 1.7%, card body Regular about 1.1%. Chart axis labels Medium about 1.0%. Cards: rounded rectangles with thin grey outline about #6B6B6B, corner radius about 1% of slide width, black fill, no shadow. Chart panel: rounded rectangle, corner radius about 1%, black fill. Lines are smooth stepped polylines with 1 to 3 px stroke. No gradients or shadows.

### Slide 25

**Text**

NVIDIA Nemotron at the Frontier
Nemotron models are the most open, post-trainable, and efficient U.S. open-weight
models on the Artificial Analysis Intelligence Index.
Delivers intelligence at ~5X faster output speed and ~30% lower cost to task
completion than the best alternative open model.
NVIDIA releases weights, training data, and recipes, so enterprises, governments,
and startups can specialize models on their own data and own the result.
Nemotron adoption is broad and accelerating across Enterprise SaaS, AI Natives/
Startups, and Sovereign.
4 out of 5 top Enterprise SaaS Platforms have deployed Nemotron models.
AI Natives and post-training partners are fine-tuning Nemotron across industries
and domains.
MODEL EVALUATION
Breaking the Performance/Cost Frontier
Performance to API Price

**Layout**

Two-column split on a black slide. The left column is a left-aligned text block, vertically centred, with the title at the top of the block and six body paragraphs below it, starting at about x 5.2% and running to about x 48.5%. The right column holds a light-cream rounded panel (x 50.7% to 95.0%, y 18.9% to 85.8%) containing the chart image, inset about 0.6% horizontally and about 8.5% from the panel top. The NVIDIA logo sits alone in the bottom-right corner. There is no slide number or footer text.

**Visuals**

One chart, a scatter plot titled 'Breaking the Performance/Cost Frontier', with eyebrow 'MODEL EVALUATION' above the title and subtitle 'Performance to API Price' below it. Y axis: 'Rubric Pass Rate', linear, ticks at 20%, 30%, 40%, 50%, 60%, 70%, 80%, 90% (no unit label beyond percent). X axis: 'Price per 1M tokens', logarithmic-looking spacing, labelled ticks $1, $4, $16, with the plot starting at about $0.1 on the left. Points, with values estimated (about) because the slide prints none: Traj. Nemotron 3 Super is a light blue-violet bubble (#B4B9F5 with dark outline) at about 67% and about $0.2, with a fading vertical trail of smaller translucent blue-violet bubbles down to about 50%. Traj. Nemotron 3 Ultra is a peach bubble (#F5C9A6 with dark outline) at about 82% and about $1.0, with a fading trail of translucent peach bubbles down to about 62%. Nemotron 3 Super is a solid grey point (#9A9A9A) at about 43% and about $0.2, labelled 'Nemotron / 3 Super'. Nemotron 3 Ultra is a solid grey point at about 52% and about $1.0, labelled 'Nemotron / 3 Ultra'. GPT-OSS 120B is a grey point at about 22% and about $0.1, labelled 'GPT-OSS 120B'. GPT 5.5 is a grey point at about 78% and about $5, labelled 'GPT 5.5'. Sonnet 4.6 is a grey point at about 85% and about $10, labelled 'Sonnet 4.6'. A dashed dark-grey frontier curve (#6B6B6B) rises from about 25% at the left edge to about 86% at the right edge, passing near the Nemotron 3 Ultra point and the GPT 5.5 point. Gridlines are light grey on a cream background (#F6F9E8); axes are dark grey lines. Labels are in a black serif face, bold for the two Traj. Nemotron titles. No legend. Highlighted points are the two Traj. Nemotron bubbles, in blue-violet and peach.

**Design**

Background: Solid black #000000 covering the full slide. Full black background (#000000). Title in NVIDIA Sans Medium, white #FFFFFF, sentence case, about 4.9% of slide height. Body in NVIDIA Sans Regular, grey #A1A1A1, about 2.0% of slide height, line spacing about 1.5 lines, paragraphs separated by about 1.5% of slide height. Accent is the NVIDIA green (#76B900) in the logo only. The chart panel is a cream rectangle #F6F9E8 with a thin light-grey border, slightly rounded corners (about 0.3% radius) and no visible shadow. Chart text uses a bold sans for the title and a serif for axis and point labels.
